# Tasks

## AS. Every level through the extractor, and what their scripts need

The extractor half (AS.1-AS.12, and the boxes split from it) stands on nothing in flight and changes no guacamole, Bevy or phone file; the wiring boxes (AS.13-AS.20 and AS.24) stand on it and on AG.19's level load, and are the TV half's. Chapter 1's parts come through byte for byte: hashed before the first code box and after the last (AS.0). Disc tests are `#[ignore]`d and read `GUAC_GIANTS_DUMP`; an extract a test writes goes under `GUAC_EXTRACT_DIR`, one level at a time, removed after, and none starts with under 40 GiB free.

- [x] AS.16 (wiring) Transitions and unloading, on AS.13: leaving a level for another takes the first's entities, colliders, assets and script run down before the next comes up, the figures on the portal carried over. Unit test: a level's entities and colliders all gone after it's left. Verify: on the Mac, chapter 1 to a second level and back three times with the process's memory back within a measured band of its first entry each time, recorded with the load. (2026-11-01, M3 Max Mac, `as-levels-tv` lane, ticked at its review with the band and the carry-over split: leaving a level takes its root and everything under it, its collision's and its props' solids and its textures, meshes and materials down as the next comes up (`receive_level`, as before AS.13), and since AS.13 a level whose scripts the TV doesn't run takes the last run down (`take_down_run`). **Unit test**: `leaving_a_level_takes_its_entities_solids_and_assets_down` (the root's tree and both solids gone a frame on, the next level up with as many meshes, images and materials as the first had and none of its solids); mutations, each killed: the root kept, the collision's solids kept, a prop's solid kept. **Measured** (`guacamole-research/as-levels-tv/runs/as16-cycles-vm/`, the binary at `3eb21d64`, chapter 1 to level 200 and back five times, each entry measured 8 s after it showed, load 6.9 at the start and 8.5 at the end): the GPU's share comes back to the byte, `footprint`'s graphics 956 MB at every chapter 1 entry and 935 MB at every level 200 one (`runs/as16-cycles/`, load 5.5-7.7); malloc's live bytes (`vmmap --summary`) at chapter 1's entries 362.7 MB first, then 379.6, 393.7, 392.4, 398.4 and 403.8 MB, its allocations 869,796, then 906,698, 942,384, 955,946, 983,907 and 1,011,030; at level 200's 279.4, 294.8, 293.8, 302.5 and 311.8 MB: about 17 MB kept on the first trip and 6-8 MB and 26,000-28,000 allocations each round trip after, where chapter 1 held as long with no trip stays flat (361.3-373.4 MB, 833,703-846,166 allocations, `runs/as16-control-vm/`, load 6.5-7.4). Resident and the footprint don't come back either: 0.95 GB resident and 1.53 GB footprint at the first entry, 1.45-1.53 GB and 1.93-2.03 GB after, the allocator keeping the freed pages (fragmentation 12 % at the first entry, about 50 % after). So the memory isn't back within a band of its first entry. Split at the review: what each round trip keeps and the band (AS.48), and leaving a level while a hero walks it, the figures carried over (AS.49). Review, 2026-11-01, M3 Max Mac: the vmmap files recomputed, chapter 1's entries grew +16.9, +14.1, -1.3, +6.0 and +5.4 MB and +36,902, +35,686, +13,562, +27,961 and +27,123 allocations, so not 6-8 MB every trip after the first; under `MallocStackLogging` (`as-levels-review/runs/heap1/`, two round trips, +17.1 MB and +53,263 live allocations) 12.0 MB and 52,953 of them are Bevy's GPU preprocessing kept for the bins the levels drew (AS.48))

- [x] AS.29 (found at AS.11) `placement.script suspended` (11 levels reach it, 63 levels' scripts name it: the challenge replays, 003's catapult master, 202's closing scene, PvP bricks): what a suspended placement's script does, read on the oracle where a level reaches it, and the VM holding and resuming it. Unit test. Verify: AS.11's rerun with it unranked. (2026-10-31, M3 Max Mac, `vm-gaps` lane: read off the EBOOT by aj-vm's way, not on the oracle, since reaching a level that suspends a script there costs a route nobody has (no route reaches PvP, a challenge, 003 or 202). What it does: the frame's scripts updater takes each placement's activity, then runs its tick unless the placement's flag 4 is set (aj-vm's and AH.59's reading of the updater, `aj-vm/FINDINGS.md`), and the member's getter and setter read and write that bit, its reset putting back the reset template's (`vm-gaps/decomp/`, outside the tree); no placement in any level file, `global.bld` or `init_setup.bld` carries it, or bit 8, in its file flags (a survey of every flag pool printing none, `vm-gaps/flags.txt`; 213's and PvP 1's flag pools all 0). So the member is the VM's own in `members::LEVELS`: on a placement the VM runs it's a flag the step's walk reads, passing a suspended placement with its place on its list, its state, locals and `system.script timer` kept until a script clears it, then ticking it at that place from the next step; set in its own tick the rest of the tick runs; a reset clears it; on anything the VM doesn't run a path through nothing. The crate docs' The tick says so, with what's a reading: a suspended placement taken off still runs its SHUTDOWN (whether the shutdown queue passes one isn't read). Being the VM's, the TV's run has it with no registration; nothing chapter 1's TV run lists reaches it. Unit tests: `a_suspended_placement_is_passed_until_its_flag_clears` (`tests/levels.rs`: PvP's bricks' shape over three placements, their ticks, clock and order) and `a_placements_script_suspended_is_the_vms` (`members.rs`). Mutations, each killed: the walk ticking a suspended placement, a reset leaving it suspended, a stranger's flag landing on the first placement, the member left to the registry, a write of 0 suspending; `vm-gaps/mutations/as29.txt`. AS.11's rerun on the 11 levels reaching it (`vm-gaps/as29-rerun.txt`): 20.5 s, load 2.96 at the start and 2.76 at the end, every chain clean with no gap at all) (2026-11-01, the vm-gaps review: read holds for what the walk does, from the EBOOT alone: the getter and setter test and flip bit 4 of a placement's flag word, the reset clears it and hands back the reset template's bit (0x8b1d0, 0x8b1f4, 0x8b220 in `vm-gaps/decomp/`), aj-vm's updater (0xac1b8) takes each placement's activity, then checks the bit just before that placement's tick, and the tick (0x6b178) is what adds the frame's seconds to `system.script timer`, so a passed placement's clock holds; the shutdown queue's pass stays the reading the crate docs mark. "From the next step" was half of it: the updater reads the flag as it comes to the placement, so one a placement ahead of it clears ticks that same step, as the VM already did; the crate docs say so now, pinned by `a_flag_cleared_ahead_of_it_in_the_walk_ticks_it_that_step`. The SHUTDOWN reading had no test (a mutation skipping it survived): `a_suspended_placement_taken_off_still_runs_its_shutdown` kills it. The flag survey again, every file printed, since the lane's skipped a file it couldn't read without a word and printed nothing either way: 145 files read, none failed, 51,686 placements, every flag word 0; `guacamole-research/vm-gaps-review/logs/flags.txt`)

- [x] AS.35 (found at AS.11) Level 214's run: the budget at `Candlestick(5)` (`Combat.ai` #1578, a loop that runs out its ops at tick 489 of the second phase) and the 1,035 s the level took, most of it every write checking every running slider (`Vm::end_slides_on`, the sampled hot spot); the loop measured on guacamole's host, the slider check indexed if it holds there. Unit test: a write's slider check against many sliders. Verify: AS.11's rerun of level 214 with its time recorded beside the load. (2026-10-31, the AS review: `end_slides_on` is the largest single path but not most of the time: a 10 s sample of level 214's run at its first stop puts `Vm::write` into it, mostly `Spot`'s comparisons, about 22% of the thread's samples, and the frame and parameter lookups (`param_frame`, `frame_cell`, `param_name`) with their SipHash hashing most of the rest, so the box measures both on guacamole's host before indexing either; `sample-214b.txt`) (2026-10-31, M3 Max Mac, `vm-gaps` lane, on the null host; the loop on guacamole's host split as AS.40: guacamole's host can't load 214 until AS.13. Measured first, by a harness outside the tree running 214's AS.11 run to B250 (`vm-gaps/tool`, `run`): 126.4 s twice, load 2.6-4.8; a 10 s sample at B150-B200 put a script write's check of every running slider (`end_slides_on` and the spots' comparisons) at 26% of the thread and std's SipHash on the VM's own maps (the macro frames, the parameters' names, the ops' state) at 22% (`vm-gaps/logs/sample-214-base.txt`). Both held, so both went: running sliders are indexed by a fingerprint of the place each eases (`slid_place`, equal for any two places a write ends a slide on), a write to a place nothing slides costing a lookup and one to a slid place comparing its own sliders alone; the VM's private maps hash through a multiply-rotate hasher of the crate's own (`ids.rs`, no new dependency), the maps its public accessors hand out keeping std's. To B250: 47.6 s (load 2.5), the sample after spread across the interpreter, the check down from 2,012 samples to 71 (`sample-214-after.txt`). What grows is the loop itself: `Combat - Check Hurt` walks `Pausable Systems Owner`'s `hurts` for every candlestick each tick, and each 50 ticks of phase B take about 3.6 s longer than the last (2.4 s to 16.7 s by B250), the null host never consuming what the set gathers, most likely, until Candlestick(5)'s loop runs out its ops at B489 as before; whether guacamole's host consumes it is AS.40's. Unit tests: `a_writes_slider_check_finds_only_the_slides_on_its_place` (`tests/slides.rs`: 300 placements sliding their own `x`, a write to one ending it alone and comparing one slider, a write to an unslid place comparing none, an ended slide off the index) and `equal_keys_hash_equal_and_ids_spread` (`ids.rs`). Mutations, each killed: a slide left off the index, an ended slider left on it, a cell's id out of its fingerprint, the index's miss scanning every live slider, the hasher unmixed; `vm-gaps/mutations/as35.txt`. AS.11's rerun of level 214 alone (`vm-gaps/as35-rerun-214.txt`): 382.6 s against AS.11's 1,035 s, load 2.92 at the start and 3.73 at the end, the same one stop, the budget at Candlestick(5) `Combat.ai` #1578, B489) (2026-11-01, the vm-gaps review: of the maps moved off SipHash the VM walks two, never the others: a returning call's frame, each cell put back to its default alone, so order-free, and `find variable`'s variables joining a placement as it's added, where two by one name on one handle (two declarations) left which holds the name to the map's order, random per process under SipHash and fixed but arbitrary under the new hasher. They join in the order made now, the later holding it as on a running placement, `of_two_variables_found_by_one_name_before_it_runs_the_later_holds_it` failing on the lane's tip one way round. Nothing draws from the generator inside a map walk; the walk's order is the lists' (`order`, the activators'), and a fingerprint's bucket is a list in the order begun whose ends commute. The fingerprint's two promises had no test, three mutations surviving (the `_otherPoint` kept in the key, kept in the fingerprint, a fingerprint match ending a slide without the exact comparison): `a_write_naming_another_point_ends_the_slide_on_its_place` and `places_sharing_a_fingerprint_end_only_the_slide_on_the_place_written` (two host places a number names, whose bits the fingerprint leaves out) kill all three. The hasher is rustc-hash 1's FxHasher, its constant 2^64 over pi made odd, not the golden ratio its comment said. Re-measured on the lane's harness built twice outside the tree: level 214 to B250 took 134.5 s at `df41c37d` and 50.1 s at the review's tree, load 3.4-4.6, the sliders running the same at every 50 ticks (47, 35, 35, 33, 34); `vm-gaps-review/logs/ab-214-*.txt`. The review's mutations, each killed by its new test, `vm-gaps-review/mutations/r2.txt`; 13 of the lane's re-run at the review's tree, each killed, `lane-spot.txt`)

When: every level's start (the level initializer's `Game - Init Minions in Level`, slot and header), a collection found, a flag set with `do save`, the header's options, the pause menu's quit; a level end saves nothing itself, marking its adventure done in `level flags` in memory for the next level's start to save. The labels at full length off the registry's live table (the table AS.34 cut at 40 characters; `au-saves/reads/save_state_labels.txt`): 0 inactive, 1 load, 2 save, 3 finalize, 4 active, 5 complete, 6-21 the device and file errors (7 invalid path, 13 read error, 14 write error, 21 general error), 22-29 the Xbox 360's login and storage ones; no script compares the state with 4 or 5. **The host** answers it (`Member::SaveState`): a 1, 2 or 3 with nothing asked reads 4 until guacamole's store has done it, then 0, or 7 for a load with no save, 14 for a write the store refused, 13 for words that don't fit the run's sets, 21 with no layout on the run; any other write kept. (2026-11-02, M3 Max Mac, as-ch1-ending lane. **The stall wasn't the thread's clock**: the cutscene system's sequence update adds the frame's game time (`combat control data`'s `game time elapsed last frame`, which `UI.ai` writes from the script timer each tick) to each thread's `thread time` while its step doesn't advance, and `CS Play Animation - Update` starts the clip once `thread time` >= the step's delay (code 2 is `>=`), which holds at a delay of 0 from the step's first tick. What stalled it: each step finds its own data among its scene object's `extra data` by looping a typed `set reference` until one takes, and the VM took any structure, so Norticus' sixth step read its dialogue's data as its animation's, the path to `animation started` went through nothing and the clip never started; the dialogue's own steps wrote their fields into the animation's data the same way. The VM now takes nothing for a structure of another definition than the right side's edit type names when that's a structure (aj-vm's OpSetReference reading, an aid: "a mismatch gives null"; a copy keeps its definition; an object against a class isn't checked). Played on, `CS Walk Obj to Location - Update` hands the engine `move to` for each Mabu (they carry no movement data of the scripts', so `Movement - Get Movement Data` returns none and the op goes to the engine): an actor the engine doesn't walk as a creature is now steered by the engine's own velocity model on its movement numbers with no body under it (`ScriptWorld::scene_move`, `move_scene_walks`: 20 ft/s, 12° a frame, at the height it stands, the block run once within the op's distance, the aim lasting the tick asked; a hero still unmodelled; stand-in ledger: `scene-walk`). The scene's animations start on its actors because the static palette, read by the game systems' placement under a tell, reads the told clone's when the placement has none (the VM tells the host what a static member is read under, `Host::told`; a file's placement told so still reads nothing: AS.80, stand-in ledger: `told-palette`). The dialogue step's update and completion each find their data among the scene object's `extra data` with a typed `set reference` loop, and the VM took the first structure there whatever its definition, so the completion read another step's data and its `dialogue ui ref` read nothing. AS.52's typed read fixes it (guac-script's `a_typed_set_reference_takes_nothing_for_another_structure` is the shape). **Headless** (`giants_level_end`'s `level_214s_intro_hands_over_on_the_ship`, sounds and the interface in, tightened): the intro's cutscene at step 5, skipped at 6, the intro off at 7 and the HUD shown at 7; no voice plays past the skip and no box is up (18.2 s, load 12.9). (2026-11-03, the au-saves review: not fixed in review, measured instead. The load can't move to the run's install: the save's sets don't exist until `Init_Setup.ai`'s STARTUP makes them (`Save - Init Savegame`) and sets every param to its default (`Save - Clear Savegame`), so it has to sit inside the run's first walk, between `init_setup.bld`'s placements and the level's, which the VM can't split today (a step stopped before a placement, or a one-step hold on the level's first placement whose native ticks it after the load: both a guac-script change and a `giants_levels` rerun). What it costs today, headless on the disc (a probe outside the commit, `au-saves-review/logs/as118-*.txt`): of the 13, 12 and 2 placements on 213, 214 and hub stage 1 running the collectible scripts that check the save in their STARTUP (`Hat_Pickup.ai`, `Story_Tablet.ai`, `Loot_Specials.ai`, `Collectable_Fairy.ai`, `UpgradeWheelPickup.ai`, `ChampionUpgradeToken.ai`), none has run it by the end of the first step: all asleep until the hero comes in range, by when the save is in. Of the 35, 35 and 22 scripts whose STARTUP does run on that step, only the init's own and `_EnemySystem_Functions.ai` call a save macro there, the latter reading `option - difficulty`, which guacamole never moves from its default 1. So no collectible comes back on those three levels; the box's verify needs a level where one wakes on the first step, or a synthetic program.) (2026-11-03, the au-level-end review: one STARTUP on the first step does read what the start sets after it: chapter 1's level definition (`_Level Defintion`) reads `Current Adventure Title`, so `level control data`'s `level adventure data` stayed empty all level; `story_save::start_run` takes it again once it names the adventure (AU.5's -1 counts). - [x] AS.139 (found at the au-hub-people review, 2026-11-06, on AU.31) A dare met isn't saved met. At chapter 1's completion screen the time dare and no lives lost read newly done, `Objective Just Complete - Level Flag Objective` has run twice, and `Save - Get/Adjust Level Saved Flags` reads `achieved target time` and `achieved no lives lost` 0 through the movie to the level change, `completed` 1 beside them; written straight through the same macro the flag reads back 1, so the store holds it and the objective's own call doesn't get there (unread: reading the flags every step moved it to 1 a step on, so the macro's static frame or its `adjust` values through `Value Set Scratch G1` are the first thing to rule out). A dare missed is saved missed either way: its check fails and no on-complete runs. Each clear reads its dares newly done, and the level's dare star doesn't add up. Verify: headless, a dare met saved met and reading done on the next clear; a dare missed left open.
  - (2026-11-06, the au-chests lane, M3 Max Mac, no RPCS3 run.) **The cause**: neither the static frame nor the `adjust` values. `System - Check Level Objective Completed` hands its objective on from the check macro's after-return code (`check struct = param<objective check struct>`), and that parameter was read with the on-complete macro's own use on top of the open calls: the VM looked at the top call alone, missed the open `Objective - Check Beat Time` under it and read the base's empty frame, so `check struct` read nothing, no `adjust` went to 1 and `Save - Get/Adjust Level Saved Flags` ran with -1s and wrote nothing (a trace: its use ran, no `Set Save Param` under it; called directly with the struct it wrote). `Vm::param_frame` now finds the nearest open call of the definition or a specialization of it (guac-script's crate docs; `a_nested_call_binds_what_an_open_specialization_returned`). That left the next clear reading newly done: `System - Set Up Level Objectives`' already-complete loop reads the save at the definition's STARTUP, a step before the TV's save is in, so `story_save::objectives_done_before` runs that loop again at the run's start (stand-in ledger: `story-save`). **Headless** (`giants_chests`' `a_dare_met_is_saved_met_and_reads_done_on_the_next_clear`, 36.2 s, load 7.7): chapter 1 in time, the time dare and no lives lost newly done, both flags 1; the next clear, on the words carried, both done from before; a late clear, the time dare open, its flag 0, the other 1. **Mutations**: the frame found at the top call alone (VM and disc), the loop not run again, every objective taken as done, an embedder's add not a combine's: all killed. Gates at AU.41.
  - (2026-11-07, the au-chests review, M3 Max Mac, no RPCS3 run.) **The rule is the original's, not a fit to one macro**: its use op points the family's INTERFACE parameters at the callee, and a use block's close points them at the nearest frame of the family down the return chain, else the base, which is `Vm::param_frame`'s search (the crate docs say so now). Those two hooks run beside the VM (a review-only patch, outside the tree) over 710 million parameter reads, 10 disc suites on guacamole's host: they part from the VM on 481 reads of the family's after-return branch, which this box didn't touch, and inside a specialization's own body, which reads its own frame. **What else now reads a different frame**: 450 of those reads, five parameters, the old rule reading an empty base each time: `returned keyframer` (352: a menu item's animation), `^return minion generic data` (59, nothing there either way), `objective check struct` (22), `adventure data` (12: the next adventure at a level's end) and `return defined menu page` (5). **The menu's reads weren't free**: a page built now reaches its focus's colour multiply, which the VM stopped on (AU.29's review). `objectives_done_before` is the original's loop op for op, run again on the save; its live checks all have level-flag overrides, so nothing done this run reads done from before. **Layout**: `1:50eea71a` as it was, `giants_saves` 3; a save with no chest flags reads every chest shut (a level's flags default 0).
